Superseded requirements are removed; detailed capability, integrity, execution, validation, optimization and reference evidence remain. Acceptance is limited to the declared stage1 target below; new stage4/5 requirements remain pending.

### Completion reporting instruction — user, 25 November 2026

When **all non-testing jobs in the agreed active scope are finished**, record a timestamped milestone here: **Implementation complete — testing pending**. List the exact remaining tests, prerequisites, whether the agent or user must run each, and their pass/fail criteria. Keep deferred work and unfinished implementation/dependencies in separate lists. Preserve unverified checks as pending and keep release/acceptance gates unchanged until those checks pass; do not infer implementation completion from a percentage or passing unit tests alone.

**Current state:** the P4-RECOVERY-02 transport/identity increment is implemented and automatically checked, but Phase4 overall has not reached this milestone. Non-testing work still includes the unimplemented relay fallback/provider boundary, ending a room when its owner is not the current coordinator (the source explicitly retains a shortcut), and any repair established by the remaining election/discovery investigation. Physical two-network/NAT/blocked-UDP/background-tab/owner-management/native-participant acceptance is separately pending. Stage6 GitHub document backup remains explicitly deferred. Mark the milestone only when the active implementation list is empty; never label those implementation gaps as merely waiting for the user to test.


## Product and delivery stages

Reuse the native CE+ editor, menus, file tree, PDF viewer and collaboration operations, including the matching dark editor and syntax colors. Preserve exact source/binary files, undo, recovery and user originals. Current standalone delivery is a browser UI in a Chrome extension; ordinary editing requires neither a server nor a native helper. LAN transport may require a small helper. Desktop/browser coverage beyond tested Chrome/macOS must be stated honestly; Windows and a VS Code extension are outside current delivery.

| Stage | What the user can do | Compilation and distribution |
|---|---|---|
| 1 — First publish: offline LaTeX editor with autocompile | Write/edit LaTeX, automatically compile source changes locally into an updated real PDF, manually recompile/stop, inspect errors, navigate exact source/PDF mappings, manage files/preferences and export/recover work. No LAN sharing in this release. | Local compilation without requiring a separate system TeX installation or server. Deliver/retain the required engine/resources locally. No distributed worker pool. |
| 2 — LAN collaboration | Share on this network; join with a link, separate passcode and session username; edit concurrently with owner-controlled continuation/copy permissions and safe reconnect. | Inherit stage1 local compilation; no separate TeX installation or distributed worker pool. Compile locally on each device from its current authorized snapshot. |
| 3 — Optional local computation and participant compilation over LAN | Participants may opt into TeX contribution; the host needs no TeX. Users may select existing Python/R/Jupyter environments for optional mathematical diagnostics, analysis and executable academic documents, including standalone offline use. | Distribute compile jobs across opted-in ready participants. Without a contributor, inherited stage1 local compilation/editing/PDF/export remain usable; additional shared native-TeX compilation reports unavailable. Research tools remain optional. |
| 4 — Internet collaboration without a server | Participants on different networks paste a shared room link into the extension, download the project snapshot and edit together with the same native session as stage2. There is no fixed host and no central server: any online peer can coordinate, and the link creator owns the room until they transfer ownership. | Every participant has a TeX distribution and compiles their own PDF locally. No compile jobs travel between peers; no central or online worker pool. |
| 5 — In-app project TeX packages | Inspect project requirements, approve installation of supported missing packages in the application, then compile and reopen offline without manual installation outside the app. | Reuse the selected browser compiler and, where supported, the selected native TeX manager through the existing helper. Depends on accepted stage1, independently of stages2–4. No new compiler or Python/R package manager. |

Stage1 is independently publishable once its offline checks/package are ready; **LAN work is not a stage1 prerequisite**. Stage2 adds collaboration/owner permissions. Stage3 adds optional shared installed-TeX compilation/research tools over LAN; stage4 does not depend on that distribution. Preserve existing work/tests; no implementation or acceptance credit changes because of renumbering. Do not implement later-stage hosting, runtime installation or new compiler comparisons as first-release work.

**23 November scope:** stage4 must provide local-first peer collaboration with no fixed application host. Stage5 is a separate package-management increment with its own acceptance checklist below; it does not reopen accepted stage1 scope. This update authorizes planning, not contacting external services, installing packages or qualifying runtime behavior.

## Effort-weighted completion ledger — baseline W3, 19 November 2026

This ledger scores the original four **product stages**; stage5 is unestimated and excluded from these subtotals. These are the user's phases1/2/3/4, separately from internal checkpoints R0–R7. Weight means estimated **total work to deliver a piece**, including its integration, error handling and scoped checks. Points are relative effort estimates, not feature/line counts, elapsed time or calibrated hours. Completion is an engineering estimate, not measured labor.

**Scope history and W3:** W1 had three stages totaling452points/80.5earned. W2 split the first stage into offline48/41.75 and LAN52/3.75, leaving optional computation210/15 and internet142/20 unchanged; the old seven-point release row was split4offline+3LAN. The user now explicitly requires first-release offline autocompile and current generated PDFs, replacing the earlier editing/existing-PDF/export-only choice. W3 adds30estimated points for local compiler/resource packaging, native autocompile/control integration and exact compiled-output fidelity/navigation. At the W3 scope baseline each new piece received25% reference-component credit because the standalone candidate disabled compilation. The implementation checkpoint below now supersedes that evidence: each compiler row now earns100% after its scoped Chrome/macOS workflow, fault and artifact checks passed; untested targets are declared separately. Stage1 is78points/78earned (100%); the current combined totals are in the table below. The scope update itself added no implementation; later actual implementation/checks changed credit. The earlier87% described editing-only W2 scope and must not be quoted for this revised first release. Preserve other rows/credit; compiler-specific packaging is in the new rows, not double-counted in the general release row.

**W5 — invitation interface moved into stage2, user 2026-11-21:** new row S2-07 (3 points, 0%) for the owner Share panel and participant Join screen; a scope effect, no delivered work changed. Stage2 52→55 points, so its percentage falls from 73.56% to 69.55% without any credit lost.

**W4 — stage4 scope replacement, user decision 20 November 2026:** the former stage4 (142 points/20 earned, central CE+ server, accounts, required online contribution, LAN-route/server fallback) is replaced entirely by serverless internet rooms; see "Stage 4 behavior". Crosswalk: none of the twelve W3 stage4 rows survives, so their 20 earned points (credit for reusable server components) are withdrawn as a scope effect, not invalidated delivery. The new stage4 totals 101 points at 0% earned: reusable stage2 transport/permission code is credited once, in stage2. Stages1–3 weights and credit are unchanged.

**Fixed credit rule:** 0%=planned/absent;25%=a relevant checked reusable component exists but the current required workflow is not integrated;50%=complete scoped workflow implemented but checks not passed;75%=integrated workflow with partial scoped checks and named gaps;100%=all checks assigned to that row pass. Documentation/installed third-party tools alone earn nothing. A partially built row stays at its lower qualifying band or is split without changing total weight. Historical reference checks cannot pass the current workflow. Separate local-piece completion from later integrated/physical/release rows; no formal gate passes because of a percentage.

**Arithmetic:** earned=sum(weight×credit/100); stage completion=earned/total stage weight×100. Combined completion sums earned and total points across the included stages before dividing; never average stage percentages. Round reported percentages and keep the raw arithmetic below. Count shared native editing/transport work once in stages1/2, scheduler/execution/common research adapters once in stage3, and additional internet/routing work once in stage4. Separate regression credit only for materially distinct integration scenarios. More stage3 effort reflects largely absent runtime confinement, math interpretation, executable documents and analysis integration; stage4 reuses prior foundations.

**Update rule:** change a piece's credit/evidence after an actual implementation/check result, then recalculate. W3 weights stay fixed; do not reweight remaining work to inflate progress. New scope or materially revised effort requires a documented new baseline/crosswalk, separating scope effects from delivered/invalidated work. A failed check lowers the affected credit with its reason. The former unweighted85%/40% estimates are not comparable baselines.

## Stage 1 behavior

**Write LaTeX and see an updated PDF.** Stage1 is a useful offline LaTeX editor, not only a text editor or an existing-PDF reader. Create/import a project, edit source, automatically compile accepted changes locally and view the newly generated real PDF. Provide manual recompile, stop/cancel, bounded diagnostics and faithful forward/inverse source navigation tied to that output's exact revision. Preserve saved autocompile Off preferences; for new projects provide autocompile by default. Coalesce/debounce edits, bound outstanding work and never publish a result for superseded source. Keep the last valid PDF on errors, visibly mark it stale when appropriate and preserve exact unsaved source.

**Runtime boundary.** No separate system TeX distribution, account, server or internet connection is required for stage1 use. Reuse the existing browser/local compiler integration and pinned resources before introducing another engine; no new compiler survey is requested. Package or explicitly prepare and retain the needed public engine/packages/fonts before claiming a project is ready offline. Browser execution still requires local resources and storage: uncached/missing packages must fail visibly without losing source/previous output, not download silently during offline use. Do not advertise unsupported packages or a first-ever disconnected startup that lacks its resources. Record actual compiler bundle/cache footprint and initial/warm latency, rather than promise a small download or instant compilation. Stage3 remains optional installed-TeX contributor distribution and research tools, not the first availability of compilation.

**Files, recovery and existing PDFs.** Keep stage1 create/import/edit/save, file/folder create/upload/rename/move/main/delete/restore, preferences, exact export/reopen and conflict/interruption safeguards. Opening an existing PDF remains supported, but identify it as supplied output: source edits update the generated compiler output only after a successful local compile, and a supplied PDF alone establishes no source mapping. Reject invalid supplied/generated outputs while preserving the last valid PDF and original data.

## Stage 2 behavior — LAN collaboration

**Prerequisite:** published/accepted stage1 offline editing. LAN sharing and participant permissions are a separate release; they must not block the first offline publish.

**Share on this network.** The owner clicks a button in the editor. Show a join link and a separate generated passcode. A participant opens the link, enters the passcode and chooses a username for this session. No account, existing LaTeX server, internet connection or TeX installation is required. The link may contain routing/public host identity, never a passcode or bearer token. Keep codes/tokens outside logs, URLs and persistent storage; validate usernames and render them as text. Show participants/carets using their session names. Stop sharing revokes access; a new room uses a new passcode. No public reachability, port forwarding or automatic listener startup.

**Owner-controlled capabilities.** The owner can set each participant to:

- **Edit while host is online** (default): edit only during the authenticated session. Host loss/disconnect locks editing until authenticated rejoin. No project persistence, recovery download, export, offline continuation or transfer into another mode. Keep pending unacknowledged edits in session memory for explicit reconciliation. Restricted source data must not enter IndexedDB, localStorage, native editor backups, service-worker caches or logs.
- **Keep a copy and continue elsewhere**: explicitly save an independent local copy, export and continue offline or in another available mode. Returning changes requires explicit comparison/merge; never overwrite the shared project automatically. Future modes remain unavailable until their stage ships.

In restricted sessions, inherited local PDF compilation must keep private source/intermediates/generated output in session memory and respect disabled export/persistence; public compiler resources may be cached separately. Do not silently persist a restricted project to make compilation work.

Only the owner grants/changes these permissions. Enforce them at host request authorization and every participant save/export/download/transfer path; hidden buttons are insufficient. Downgrades stop future copying but cannot recall previously retained copies. These are application permissions: participants who can read source can manually copy it outside the app. Owner originals and authorized independent copies remain recoverable.

## Stage 3 behavior

**LAN-COMPILE-01 — pending; depends on accepted stage 2.** Add participant-only opt-in controls, local TeX selection/readiness and contribution status. Reuse `runtime/chrome-extension/native/src/tex.rs` confinement/cancellation and the existing `runtime/participant-compile/` job lifecycle where compatible; do not replace either with another queue or engine without a demonstrated gap. The owner's coordination role does not make the host a required TeX worker. Selection means an explicit participant choice of a local installation, with packages supplied separately; do not download/install TeX silently.

Prepare immutable source/assets/compiler manifests, bounded authenticated transfer, fair job dispatch, leases/heartbeats, cancellation, disconnect retry and stale-result refusal. Only ready opted-in participants receive jobs. Turning contribution off stops new claims and safely releases/cancels assigned work. A job result must match the requested project/revision/resources/engine before publication; preserve the last valid PDF/source on failure. Distribute independent compile jobs; do not promise that a single TeX process is split across computers. Make any compiler/package mismatch visible rather than silently changing engines.

Implementation paths: existing LAN transport/owner authorization, extension worker UI/native bridge, `runtime/chrome-extension/native/src/{main.rs,tex.rs}`, `runtime/participant-compile/{bootstrap.js,client.mjs,host.mjs}`, existing browser and artifact tests. First inspect actual interfaces and document exact commands; historical native/pool passes do not qualify this integration. Timebox interface discovery to 30 minutes, then implement the complete opt-in→assigned job→validated PDF action before one focused check pass.

Pass: host without TeX plus two participant contexts (one contributor, one noncontributor) collaborate; only opted-in workers claim shared contributor jobs; inherited local compilation remains available to each user; a second opt-in participant joins distribution fairly; opt-out/loss/cancel/retry cannot duplicate jobs or publish stale results; no contributors report shared compilation unavailable while stage1 local compilation remains usable; missing packages preserve source/prior output. Check malformed files, unauthorized jobs/results, confinement, exact multi-file/math/bibliography/PDF/source mapping and fairness under concurrent jobs. Measure request→visible PDF, queue wait, bytes, memory and repeated use on the same fixtures/machine. Record real two-device acceptance separately. On failure keep stage 1 usable and shared compilation unavailable; preserve originals/previous PDF and repair the integration. Rollback disables contribution/dispatch, not editing or saved projects.

### Optional local research tools — stage 3 scope

**User addition, 19 November 2026:** add Python, R and installed Jupyter kernels as optional local integrations alongside optional TeX. Prioritize **mathematical error detection** and **executable academic documents**. Users select their existing environments and enable only the features they want. Offline editing/LAN and TeX contribution must still work when Python, R, Jupyter or any analysis package is absent or disabled. No subscription, paid API, cloud computation, bundled language distribution or mandatory installation. A feature may require its corresponding user-installed runtime/kernel/packages; show what is unavailable without installing anything automatically. Optional research execution also works on standalone local projects, without a host or LAN.

| Integration | Required user-visible scope | Dependency and honesty boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Python | Diagnose supported algebraic errors/invalid transformations; derivatives, integrals, limits, equation solving and optimization; inspect undefined variables, inconsistent notation and supported contradictions; analyze datasets and generate LaTeX-ready figures/tables/results. | Use the selected Python environment and available libraries. SymPy is a reuse candidate for symbolic work, not a new theorem prover. Inventory installed numerical/data/plotting packages before selecting adapters; no bundled scientific stack or automatic package additions. Unsupported/unevaluated computations must stay explicit. |
| R | Regression, panel-data and time-series analysis; hypothesis tests, confidence intervals and statistical computations; tables/figures linked to data/model inputs. | Use the selected existing R installation and its installed packages. Reuse standard R/statistics facilities and project packages where adequate; a missing panel/time-series dependency disables that action with a clear explanation rather than choosing a different model silently. |
| Jupyter | Execute Python/R cells inside the existing editor, inspect inline text/figures/tables, interrupt/restart a kernel, and keep prose, code, inputs and output provenance in one project. | Jupyter provides kernel execution/communication rather than another language. Require an installed compatible kernel for the selected language; an R executable alone does not establish that an R Jupyter kernel is installed. Reuse the installed Jupyter client/kernel machinery instead of building a notebook server, editor or kernel. |

**Mathematical diagnostics, not formal verification.** Check only explicitly supported expressions/transformations with visible variable declarations, scope and assumptions (real/complex, sign, nonzero denominators, dimensions where supported). Report the source range, parsed interpretation, assumptions, diagnostic and reason/counterexample. Separate **issue found**, **no issue found in the supported check**, **inconclusive** and **unsupported syntax**; never show a global “mathematically correct” badge. Numerical sampling can reveal a counterexample, but cannot certify an identity. Include cancellation/domain exclusions, square roots, branch-sensitive functions, bound variables and ambiguous notation in checks. Undefined-symbol/notation findings are suggestions until project declarations/context establish an error; do not treat every unfamiliar macro or index as a mistake. Contradiction detection is limited to supported explicit constraints, not unrestricted academic reasoning. Never rewrite source automatically.

**Executable documents and generated outputs.** Reuse native source editing/completion and place execution/results controls in that UI; do not add a second notebook editor. Keep explicit cell/script identity, language/kernel, ordered dependencies and the source/data/environment revision used for each result. Store code and user-approved outputs in the project with exact export/reopen behavior and relative LaTeX references. A local computation may generate an insertable scalar, a table fragment or a figure; stage/validate outputs and ask for an explicit insert/replace action rather than rewriting the manuscript or overwriting arbitrary paths. Bind outputs to exact code/data/model inputs; mark them stale when inputs change. PDF compilation is an inherited stage1 local action, with optional shared contributor compilation added in stage3; a runtime result must not imply that an updated PDF has compiled.

**Dynamic analysis.** Initial execution is manual. Users may explicitly enable recomputation for a trusted project; invalidate downstream outputs on code/data/model changes, debounce/coalesce work, detect dependency cycles and never execute an entire project silently on open/import/join. Use the existing job identity/cancel/stale-result principles for research tasks, with a local execution adapter; do not enroll Python/R kernels in the LAN/online worker pool just because a participant contributed TeX. Local code/data stay local unless the user explicitly shares permitted inputs/results. A host or collaborator cannot select another device's environment, execute code on it or receive its datasets by granting room editing access.

**Execution and permission boundaries.** Imported/collaborator code and rich outputs are untrusted. Require explicit per-project execution consent, keep environment selection device-local, launch the intended interpreter directly (not a shell command assembled from source), and use recoverable project copies with bounded input/output, runtime/memory/process limits, cancellation and child cleanup. Do not assume TeX's sandbox safely supports general Python/R/kernel execution: inspect actual kernel subprocess/filesystem/network needs and qualify the chosen confinement before enabling it. Inherited API credentials, arbitrary home-directory access and outbound connections are not granted by selecting a runtime. Handle legitimate additional data-file permissions through explicit user selection, with no implicit broad filesystem grant. If safe confinement is unsupported, keep that execution path unavailable rather than silently run unrestricted.

Prefer the existing native bridge/private IPC to expose only the required local operations. If an installed Jupyter server is needed, use authenticated loopback-only access, validated origin/session and bounded messages; never expose its kernel ports over LAN or put its connection credentials in URLs, persistent browser storage, logs, exported projects or UI bundles. Do not forward raw startup banners that contain connection tokens. Render rich outputs as sanitized inert content with size limits; never execute notebook JavaScript/HTML or TeX shell-escape as an output-rendering shortcut. Owner permissions remain binding: a restricted participant cannot gain local persistence/export through notebooks, generated artifacts or recovery. Use session-only execution if it can uphold those limits; otherwise refuse that participant execution path visibly without granting continuation.

**Reproducibility and integrity.** Record safe project-level provenance: code/input digests, execution order, runtime/kernel/package versions, supplied random seeds and output digests, without credentials, full environment dumps or private absolute paths. Support a fresh-kernel ordered rerun and show missing dependencies/state explicitly. Preserve data/code originals and prior valid outputs on execution failure, interrupt, timeout, revoked access or interrupted publication. Numerical results can vary with environment and model assumptions; do not promise cross-machine bitwise equality or infer a valid statistical specification from a successful run. No private datasets, notebook payloads or results enter public fixtures/plans/logs.

### Stage 3 research implementation milestones

Shared prerequisites: accepted stage2 release; explicit synthetic project copies; existing optional native/runtime bridge; selected user-installed environments only. No real dataset, environment change, package installation or new listener is authorized by this planning addition. Discover actual runtime discovery/build/test commands from the code and selected environment before recording runnable commands; do not invent a “check math” CLI or assume a kernel is installed. Proposed paths are extension settings/native bridge, project execution/result adapters, existing native editor integration and focused tests; inspect actual symbols before creating modules. Compare existing helpers, platform process controls and installed maintained packages proportionally, and record the choice/footprint/licensing here before substantial code.

| Step / dependencies | Complete user milestone and implementation actions | Pass/fail, pace and rollback |
|---|---|---|
| RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01 / shared prerequisites; reuses LAN-COMPILE-01 runtime-selection pattern, does not require TeX | Add device-local descriptive choices for Python environment, R installation and available Jupyter kernels, feature toggles/readiness, guarded execution/cancel, bounded result handling and no-autorun project consent. Reuse installed Jupyter libraries for protocol operations; Rust remains the custom native process boundary, with minimal Python/R code only for those runtimes' APIs. | Timebox interface discovery to30minutes, then implement the complete select→explicit run→result/interrupt workflow before one check pass. With all tools absent/disabled, stage1 editing/export and stage3 TeX still work; selecting one tool enables only its supported actions. Test wrong executable/kernel, missing package, hostile imported code/output, timeout, worker crash, path/network/secret denial and permissions. On failure keep connector disabled, preserve projects/prior outputs and repair confinement; rollback feature settings/bridge only, never the user's environment. |
| MATH-CHECK-01 / RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01, selected Python and available symbolic library; priority addition | Implement a declared supported LaTeX/expression subset, explicit symbol/assumption context, range-bound diagnostics, transformation comparison, computations and visible inconclusive/unsupported states. Do not call evaluation-based expression parsers directly on arbitrary document text; use a constrained validated representation and treat unsupported macros as unsupported. Never run code embedded in math or replace the native editing/parser pipeline wholesale. | Implement the full select-equation→assumptions→diagnostic/result action, then check synthetic correct/incorrect/domain-sensitive transformations, undefined/bound/index symbols, inconsistent notation, explicit contradictory constraints, malformed macros and expensive expressions. Include examples such as cancellation at zero and sqrt(x²) without a sign assumption. Check displayed explanation/source range and exact unchanged manuscript. Timebox first supported-slice implementation to one focused session; record supported coverage, unresolved cases and measured false positives/latency, not a universal accuracy claim. Failure preserves source and returns inconclusive/unsupported; disable defective rules rather than label ambiguity an error. |
| DOCEXEC-01 / RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01 and an installed compatible Python or R Jupyter kernel; priority addition | Run cells/scripts from the native editor; show bounded sanitized outputs, inline figures/tables, stop/restart, execution order/provenance and explicit LaTeX insertion. Implement notebook/project import/export only through the existing guarded file paths; never execute on import. Bind result publication to code/data/environment revisions and support fresh-kernel ordered rerun. | One complete usable implementation session target, revised if an actual protocol/confinement prerequisite blocks it. Then check Python and R with their respective installed kernels, missing kernel, out-of-order hidden state, restart/rerun, error/interrupt/timeout, concurrent source edits, malicious rich output and reload/export fidelity. Restricted participants cannot persist/export through execution; permitted copies remain independent. Pass requires actual installed-kernel execution, not mocked protocol messages. Failure keeps exact code/data/previous outputs and marks results stale/failed; rollback execution/renderer integration without replacing the editor. |
| ANALYSIS-01 / RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01; generation/provenance UI from DOCEXEC-01 | Add Python dataset/calculus/optimization helpers and R regression, panel/time-series, tests/confidence-interval helpers using selected installed capabilities. Reuse the same generated-result pipeline for tables/figures/scalars; explicitly declared model/data dependencies drive optional trusted-project refresh. Present model/formula/sample/missing-data choices, package/version and failures rather than silently changing analysis. | Implement a complete data→analysis→preview→explicit manuscript insertion→recompute workflow before checks. Use small synthetic datasets with independently specified expected fits/statistics, domain/solver failures, missing values, model/sample changes and input mutation during execution. Verify only downstream outputs become stale/update; cycles do not run indefinitely; numeric tables/figure references/export are correct and originals unchanged. One session target per coherent analysis family, not an unbounded package catalog. On failure retain previous outputs as stale, show the failed dependency/model and stop dependent publication. |

Stage3 research check pass: run the implemented local workflows with all application internet requests blocked; measure runtime discovery/startup, trivial equation/cell response, repeated output rendering, cancellation, peak process memory, project/generated bytes and added installed/archive size on the same fixture/machine. Define budgets from the selected actual environments before accepting the implementation; package size alone is not a performance result. Keep interpreters/libraries/kernels out of the application bundle and verify missing/disabled tools trigger no startup/import overhead beyond feature detection explicitly requested by the user. Preserve all test code, synthetic fixtures and reference results. Stage3 acceptance must exercise offered integrations on declared installed environments and prove the absent-tool/offline baseline, without forcing any end user to install them. These additions do not hold up stage1 publication.

Primary integration references checked19November2026: [SymPy parsing and parser cautions](https://docs.sympy.org/latest/modules/parsing.html), [SymPy assumptions and conditional simplification](https://docs.sympy.org/latest/guides/assumptions.html), [Jupyter kernels and connection files](https://jupyter-client.readthedocs.io/en/stable/kernels.html), [Jupyter server execution/authentication boundary](https://jupyter-server.readthedocs.io/en/latest/operators/security.html), [IRkernel requirements/registration](https://irkernel.github.io/installation/). These establish integration constraints, not evidence that this application's runtime selection, analysis or diagnostics exist. No new dependency is selected or installed by this plan update.

## Stage 4 behavior — internet rooms without a server

**Decision — user, 20 November 2026 (Europe/Madrid).** This section replaces the former central-server stage4 entirely (online CE+ service, accounts, required online work contribution, LAN-route preference/server fallback). User direction: "there will be no shared internet. So we're going to assume all users will have the overleaf extension + a server link"; "I do not want a host. Anyone can be a host with the shared link"; "the creator of the link can be assumed as the owner first and later it can be changed by the owner themselve". Chosen options: replace the old design entirely; optional backup owner; every participant has a TeX distribution and compiles their own PDF. **Unresolved:** the peer-discovery (signaling) mechanism. Default candidate is existing free public signaling through a maintained library, so nothing is operated by us; an own minimal relay is the fallback. Stop and ask before the first connection to any external service.

**User flow.**
1. **Share:** the creator clicks Share. The extension creates a random room ID, room encryption material and an owner key pair. The link contains only public routing/room identity. Enter the separate passcode inside the extension, as in stage2; establish room keys over an authenticated encrypted exchange. No room key, passcode or bearer/session token belongs in a URL, including its fragment, logs or localStorage. A room ID alone grants no access. This supersedes the earlier secret-in-fragment design under the user's credential-placement rules.
2. **Join:** a participant pastes the link and passcode into the extension, finds the online peers, downloads a digest-verified project snapshot (source and binary assets), then joins the live session with stage2 native OT, carets and session names.
3. **Coordination:** CE+ OT needs one party to order edits. The first online peer coordinates; failover must establish one authenticated coordinator epoch and a shared confirmed history, then deduplicate resubmitted operations. A highest-version number alone does not establish a common history or prevent split brain. With nobody online, users permitted to retain copies reopen/edit locally and explicitly reconcile at the next meeting. Restricted participants retain stage2's session-only storage and owner-online editing limits; coordinator election cannot grant offline continuation or ownership.
4. **Ownership:** the creator is the owner. The owner signs the member/role list (stage2 grants); every peer verifies the signature chain before accepting edits or role changes, so a coordinator cannot promote itself. Transfer: the owner signs a transfer to a member, who accepts by signing; signatures from a superseded owner are refused. The owner may name one backup member who can claim ownership if the owner key is lost; without one, permissions freeze while editing continues. Revocation: a new signed list plus room-key rotation; the UI states that a revoked member keeps the copy already downloaded.
5. **Forks:** copies edited apart (separate groups or offline peers) are detected on reconnect and go through explicit reconciliation with both versions retained; never an automatic overwrite.
6. **Compilation:** each peer compiles its own PDF with the inherited stage1 compiler or its selected installed TeX. Missing packages are shown; no compile job leaves the peer.

**Carried forward verbatim (restored 21 November 2026 from the retired Kod plan copy); still binding on every stage4 piece.** Under the serverless design read "the server" as any peer, coordinator, signaling or relay service; ONLINE-01/ONLINE-LAN-01 map to the P2P milestones, S4-12 to S4-09/P2P-FINAL-01. Stage4 sends no compile jobs between peers, so the job-sandbox and enrollment bullets apply only where stage3 contribution is used; the rest apply in full.

**ONLINE-SEC-01 — user requirement, 19 November 2026; binding on ONLINE-01, ONLINE-LAN-01 and every stage4 piece.** Assume the server, its operator account or any member is hostile. The worst permitted outcome is loss, leak or alteration of project documents held by the server; no participant PC may be compromised and no local file that the user has not deliberately uploaded may be read, listed, written or executed through the server or another member. Everything received from the server or a peer (source, assets, job manifests, results, messages, scripts, URLs, file names) is untrusted data. Enforce on the client; server checks do not count:
- **No server code in privileged contexts.** Extension pages run only packaged code (`script-src 'self'`); never load or `eval` server-supplied script/HTML there. Keep `externally_connectable` absent so a server-served page cannot message the extension or helper. Native messaging stays restricted to the extension origin and fixed commands; no command may take a server-chosen absolute path, executable, argument list or environment.
- **Untrusted compilation only in a qualified sandbox.** Server/peer jobs run in BusyTeX inside a browser Worker (network limited to packaged public assets) or in the native helper's existing deny-default `sandbox-exec` profile (`runtime/chrome-extension/native/src/tex.rs`: reads only system paths, the selected TeX tree and the job directory; writes only the job directory; no network; `-no-shell-escape`, `openin_any/openout_any=p`, cleared environment). Never run received jobs with an unconfined system TeX; platforms without a qualified sandbox (currently all except macOS) cannot contribute native jobs. Bound CPU time, memory, disk and output size per job so required contribution cannot exhaust the PC.
- **No server-provisioned executables.** Runtime "provisioning/enrollment" (S4-05) may not download or install binaries, WASM, packages or TeX trees chosen by the server. Executable assets come only from the signed extension/helper package, pinned by digest in that package; updates come only through the store/signed release channel.
- **Python/R/Jupyter stay local and off the online path.** Never enrolled in online work; never run because content arrived, opened or synced. Execution consent binds to the exact code digest and is asked again after any remote change. Remote members cannot trigger, select or observe another device's execution. Execution remains unavailable unless the confinement qualified in RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01 denies home-directory reads, credentials and network.
- **Local files only by explicit user action.** Uploads only from files/folders the user picks for that project; no background directory access, persistent broad file handles or server-requested reads. Downloads/exports are written only to a user-chosen location, file names sanitized (no paths, `..`, control or reserved names), archives checked against zip-slip/zip-bomb limits, nothing auto-opened or executed. Render PDFs with `isEvalSupported: false` (already set in `runtime/browser-recovery/`) and rich outputs as inert sanitized content.
- **Peer/LAN route.** The direct route carries only bounded, authenticated sync/job messages; the helper exposes no file-serving, shell or debug endpoint and listens only while a session is active.
- **Tokens.** Online session tokens are scoped to the server, short-lived, never in URLs/localStorage/logs; a server breach must not yield credentials usable on the PC or other services.

Pass (part of S4-12; no stage4 acceptance without it): with a deliberately malicious test server and member, attempt to read canary files planted in the test user's home (e.g. `~/canary-secret.txt`, `~/.ssh/canary`) via `\input`/`\openin`/`\includegraphics`, absolute and `../` paths, symlinks inside uploaded archives, shell-escape and `\write18`, network exfiltration from a job, oversized/zip-bomb archives, malicious file names on export, crafted PDFs/rich outputs, forged native-helper messages, a server-supplied runtime/binary, and remote-triggered Python/R execution. Pass only if no canary content or listing reaches the server, no file outside the job/chosen locations is written, nothing executes outside the sandbox, and resource caps hold. A failure blocks internet mode; it does not affect stages 1–3.

**Not allowed:** running, deploying or paying for any server; storing project content anywhere except participants' computers; sending the room key, passcode or source in clear to signaling/relay services; replacing native OT with a CRDT; sending compile jobs between peers; first use of an external public service without user authorization. **Stop and ask:** before choosing/first contacting a signaling service; if loss-free handover is not achievable with the reused OT; if Chrome lacks a required WebCrypto signature algorithm.

**P2P-ROOM-01 — pending; depends on accepted stage2 (CEX-LAN-01/PERM-01).** Deliver flow steps 1, 2 and 6 with the first peer as fixed coordinator. Reuse stage2 framing/AES-GCM/replay checks, `lan-room.mjs` passcodes and session names, native OT and the loopback harness (`test/lan-loopback-*.mjs`); verify actual paths/symbols first. Discovery, timeboxed to 30 minutes: compare browser WebRTC data channels (platform facility) with maintained signaling libraries and an own relay for footprint, licence and reliability; record the choice here. **Discovery result, 23 November 2026 (read-only, within the 30-minute timebox; no external contact):** existing LAN code is `runtime/chrome-extension/lan-room.mjs` (passcode proof, P-256 ECDH, HKDF salted with passcode, AES-GCM counters, owner authorizes in its own browser) and `runtime/chrome-extension/lan/src/main.rs` (relay that only forwards bytes). Its payloads are already end-to-end encrypted, so a relay sees ciphertext only. Extension CSP is `connect-src 'self' blob:`, so internet signaling needs a manifest change naming the chosen host. Options: (a) browser WebRTC `RTCDataChannel` (platform, no new dependency) with the existing room protocol on top and a small own WebSocket signaling relay carrying only SDP/ICE; (b) maintained WebRTC library (adds dependency and usually a public signaling service); (c) own ciphertext relay only (always-on hosted service, no NAT traversal). **Recommendation: (a).** Two external items need the user's approval before any contact or deployment: a signaling host, and STUN/TURN providers (TURN is the ciphertext fallback for blocked UDP, needs a hard spending cap). Pass: two profiles on different networks (or simulated NAT) join by link and converge; wrong/expired/forged links or passcodes are denied; a malformed snapshot is refused without writes; the room key and passcode are absent from captured signaling/relay payloads and logs. Failure: keep stage2 LAN working and disable internet sharing.

**P2P-OWNER-01 — pending; depends on P2P-ROOM-01.** Deliver flow step 4. Reuse the stage2 grant checks and add signature/chain verification with platform WebCrypto; the owner private key is non-extractable and never in exports or logs. Pass: transfer with acceptance, backup claim, refusal of revoked, forged, replayed and superseded-owner signatures, and no coordinator self-promotion. Failure: freeze permission changes, keep editing under the last valid list.

**P2P-HANDOVER-01 — pending; depends on P2P-OWNER-01.** Deliver flow steps 3, 5 and S4-07. Pass: with three peers typing, killing the coordinator loses and duplicates no edit and carets recover; two groups edit apart and reunite with the fork detected and explicitly merged, both versions retained; all peers offline then one reopens and edits. This is a data-loss path: run crash/interruption tests. Failure: pause room editing, keep local candidates and export, never auto-merge.

**P2P-FINAL-01 — pending; depends on the three milestones above.** Packaged extension with discovery-library licence/notices, the user's secret/provenance checks, and two physical computers on different networks (for example home Wi-Fi and a phone hotspot). Run the ONLINE-SEC-01 canary/malicious-peer test with a hostile peer and hostile signaling/relay in place of the malicious server; a failure blocks internet mode. Measure join time, edit-to-peer latency, relay share and memory. Initial targets, unmeasured: a 5 MB fixture snapshot joins within 10 s on ordinary broadband; direct-path edit-to-peer p95 ≤300 ms; handover pause ≤5 s. Record misses and causes; no correctness weakening to meet them.

### Stage 4 required test scenarios — addition, 23 November 2026

All four P2P milestones remain pending. Use synthetic projects and the existing native editor/OT, LAN permission and snapshot fixtures. Implement each whole user workflow first, run its checks once, then repair failures. A simulated network test cannot replace final physical two-network acceptance. Signaling/STUN/relay infrastructure may be necessary even without an application server; record the selected provider, metadata exposure, retention, quotas, availability and costs before claiming serverless operation. No provider or relay is selected or authorized by this plan update. No durable project storage at a third party, mandatory paid service or public room directory.

| Test ID / assigned milestone | User action and required evidence | Failure action |
|---|---|---|
| P2P-T01 / P2P-ROOM-01 | Share, join from another network, receive exact Unicode/source/binary snapshot, edit concurrently, undo locally and see named carets; compile a real local PDF with exact revision/SyncTeX identity. Wrong passcode, unknown member and tampered/missing/oversized snapshot fail without project writes. Capture traffic to verify encryption and absence of credentials/source in signaling payloads. | Refuse join, retain originals and last valid PDF; keep LAN mode available. |
| P2P-T02 / P2P-ROOM-01 | Exercise direct connection, NAT traversal, approved ciphertext relay fallback, blocked UDP, discovery outage and relay outage. Show which path is active and explain unreachable rooms. Verify bounded queued bytes/backpressure under a slow peer, packet loss and reordered/duplicate messages. No silent public listener, payment or cleartext fallback. | Show unavailable/reconnecting and retain authorized pending work; never claim universal NAT reachability. |
| P2P-T03 / P2P-OWNER-01 | Creator transfers ownership with recipient acceptance; reject forged/stale membership, old-owner signatures, coordinator self-promotion and two competing transfers. Owner reload preserves usable protected identity; lost-key/backup claims follow one documented acceptance rule. Partition alone cannot prove owner-key loss or justify a takeover. | Freeze permission changes under the last verified authority; no automatic promotion. |
| P2P-T04 / P2P-OWNER-01 | Remove a peer while online and while partitioned; rotate keys and reject its reconnect, old invitations and replayed frames. Apply signed permission downgrades before further operations. Scan restricted-session storage/cache/export/recovery/compiled outputs for synthetic source markers. State that already downloaded copies cannot be recalled. | Deny affected access/persistence; retain owner data and independently authorized copies. |
| P2P-T05 / P2P-HANDOVER-01 | Three peers type during graceful departure, coordinator crash and crash immediately before/after acknowledgement. Verify exact expected text/history, stable operation IDs, one active epoch, no lost/duplicate acknowledged operation and recoverable unacknowledged edits. A stale coordinator must not overwrite the new history. | Pause ambiguous shared editing; preserve candidates and permitted exports for review. |
| P2P-T06 / P2P-HANDOVER-01 | Partition into two editing groups, make text and create/rename/delete/binary changes, reunite, detect divergent histories and explicitly reconcile. Close all peers, reopen an authorized local copy offline, edit and later rejoin. Restricted users remain locked and cannot persist a copy. | Retain both authorized versions; never silently overwrite or infer permission from possession. |
| P2P-T07 / P2P-FINAL-01 | Run ONLINE-SEC-01 against malicious peer/coordinator/signaling/relay, including forged helper messages, canary-file reads, resource exhaustion and remotely triggered research/package installation. Neither a join nor a received project may execute code, install resources or send a compile job to another peer. | Block internet acceptance on any boundary failure; preserve passing offline/LAN scope. |
| P2P-T08 / P2P-FINAL-01 | Packaged extension on two physical computers on different networks; repeat join/edit/leave/rejoin/handover and local compile. Record exact runtime versions, snapshot size, join time, edit latency p95, handover pause, relay bytes, peak memory and short repeated-use trend against the targets above. Run existing offline/LAN regressions once. | Record unsupported paths and missed budgets; repair correctness failures before acceptance. |

Execution: working directory is this repository, Node >=22 and the existing disposable-browser fixture runtime. Before implementation, inspect `runtime/chrome-extension/{lan-room,lan-sync,lan-transfer}.mjs`, `runtime/browser-recovery/native-workspace.mjs` and actual `test/browser/` entrypoints; extend existing harnesses rather than invent a second transport. Record the exact implemented targeted commands here; the P2P test IDs above are acceptance scenarios, not existing runnable scripts. Interface investigation is limited to30minutes per milestone; then implement or record the concrete unresolved prerequisite. Compare the candidate and previous version on the same fixture/machine in the final check pass, including artifact size and memory; unavailable prior internet behavior is reported as absent, never as a speedup. Preserve copies before storage/protocol migrations. Rollback disables internet mode and retains recoverable source/history; it must not downgrade existing project data. External-provider selection/first contact and loss-free OT infeasibility retain the stop points above.

## Stage 5 — project TeX package management, pending

**Objective and scope, user 23 November 2026:** users manage the LaTeX packages required by their projects inside the application, without manually installing them elsewhere. Provide a native Packages action showing detected requirements, installed/available/missing/unsupported status, download/storage size and an explicit Install action with progress/cancel/retry. Successful installation makes the same project compile and reopen offline. Include package files, dependencies and required fonts/data/formats where the selected compiler supports them. No compiler replacement, full distribution installer, remote execution, Python/R/Jupyter dependency management, silent installation on import/join or changes to unrelated environments. Project diagnostics are hints until resolved against compiler/package metadata: dynamic TeX macros prevent a source scan alone from proving all dependencies.

**Prerequisites and verified boundary:** accepted stage1 and its existing compiler/resources/store are the foundation; stage2–4 are independent. Historical native package additions and bundled BusyTeX assets do not establish an in-app installation API. Browser add-on loading, compatible metadata, trusted source, per-package licensing and transactional activation remain unresolved. Native support additionally requires the selected stage3 helper/TeX integration; browser package management must not require it. Use an app-owned resource tree first; never mutate system TeX or a user environment implicitly. The approved package source is application-controlled and pinned/verified, never taken from a manuscript URL, peer manifest or custom remote command. No privileged post-install scripts, shell escape, executable updates, helper/WASM downloads or arbitrary package-manager arguments; executable changes require the signed application update channel. Ordinary TeX source packages still compile only inside the qualified sandbox.

**TEX-PKG discovery, 2026-11-23 ~17:55 CET (user: "manage phase 5 now").** Verified from `~/Downloads/LaTeX_Local_LAN_Test/recovery/busytex` and `~/overleaf-s3` @ 3bfc1d2:
- The compiler is BusyTeX (texlyre build, TeX Live 2026, emscripten 5.0.4). It preloads `texlive-basic` and loads `texlive-recommended`/`texlive-extra` on demand from `.providespackage.txt` catalogs; together these are 635 MB and include collection-latexextra. Full file lists are in `texlive-*.txt`.
- `runtime/participant-compile/worker.js` refuses every network request except the pinned bundle files, so the compiler itself never downloads.
- `project-compile.mjs` passes project files as `additionalFiles`; the pipeline writes them into a fresh MEMFS `/home/web_user/project_dir` for each compile.
- `texmf.cnf`: `TEXMFHOME` (default `~/texmf`) is searched without an ls-R database (no `!!`), so a TDS tree there needs no index rebuild.
- Proposed design: installed package files live in an app-owned versioned store (IndexedDB, per package and revision, content-addressed). Each compile injects the project's resolved set under `project_dir/.texmf/…`, and the worker sets `TEXMFHOME` there, which gives an exact resource manifest per compile and nothing persistent in the compiler FS.
- Metadata and integrity: the TeX Live 2026 `texlive.tlpdb` carries runfiles, `depend`, `containerchecksum` (SHA-512), size and `catalogue-license` per package. It is signed (`texlive.tlpdb.sha512.asc`) with the TeX Live key, pinned in the app, so mirrors are only transport. Archives are `archive/<pkg>.tar.xz`, so xz decoding is needed in the browser.
- Not activatable in the browser compiler (shown as unsupported there): packages whose tlpdb `execute` lines need font maps (`addMap`/`addMixedMap`), formats (`AddFormat`) or hyphenation (`AddHyphen`).
- Stop point reached: first contact with a package source, the xz dependency choice and the Packages UI placement need the user.

**Stage 5 decisions and execution, 2026-11-23 ~18:10 CET.** User choices: package source is CTAN mirrors via mirror.ctan.org. The Packages screen is a Settings tab plus an "Install missing packages" button on compile errors. Installer: "whichever is simplest, least code", so the browser fetches, verifies and unpacks with a vendored `xzwasm` 0.1.2 (MIT, 46 KB) and no helper is needed. Native `tlmgr` (TEX-PKG-03) is declared unsupported. **2026-11-24:** tlmgr stays unused; S5-06 instead gives the chosen native TeX the browser-verified packages as a per-job app-owned tree (coordinator default, user may change). Measured: 2,406 of 4,645 TeX Live 2026 packages (1,363 MB) are absent from the bundles; of those, 273 need font maps, 16 formats and 1 hyphenation, all unsupported in the browser. `mirror.ctan.org` redirects each request to a different mirror, and two mirrors were seen out of sync, so one mirror is resolved per catalog refresh and every file comes from it. Activation: our worker passes `texmf_local = [project_dir/.texmf]` (the pipeline adds it to TEXMFDIST, which needs `ls-R`), and each compile injects the installed files plus a generated `ls-R`.
- S5-1 (verified 2026-11-24, work/stage5 @ 2d6a53d): pure modules `runtime/packages/{tlpdb,tar,openpgp}.mjs` with node tests. tlpdb parsing, dependency closure, file→package index and browser support; bounded ustar reader that refuses traversal/absolute/links/oversize; detached RSA signature check against the pinned `texlive.asc` (fixture: real `texlive.tlpdb.sha512` and `.asc`).
- S5-2 (verified 2026-11-24, work/stage5 @ 2d6a53d): `runtime/packages/store.mjs`: IndexedDB catalog and package store, install flow (resolve mirror, fetch, SHA-512, xz, tar, licence, activate atomically), cancel, retry, uninstall; detection from sources and compile logs.
- S5-3 (verified 2026-11-24, work/stage5 @ 2d6a53d): compile integration (`worker.js` texmf_local, `project-compile.mjs` injection and fingerprint), Packages tab and compile-error button, manifest CSP `connect-src https:`, `package.mjs`.
- S5-4 (verified 2026-11-24, work/stage5 @ 2d6a53d): `test/browser/packages.browser.mjs` PKG-T01/T02/T03/T05/T06 on the real mirror (approved), one check pass, then the ledger.

### Package implementation milestones and tests

Shared setup: this repository; Node >=22; existing synthetic multi-file/font/bibliography projects; disposable browser profiles; no real project/environment mutation. Inspect the current compiler and package/resource APIs before recording exact build/test commands. Reuse existing storage, integrity, native bridge and bounded download/extraction helpers. Compare those facilities with the selected distribution's maintained package manager only for the actual missing capability; record compatibility, dependency/installed footprint, licensing and copies/I/O here. Timebox interface discovery to30minutes, then implement the entire milestone before its check pass. Do not start another engine comparison. Each new branch/installer boundary needs focused tests in the existing test harness; the IDs below specify planned checks, not completed test code.

| Step / dependencies / relevant paths | Whole user action and implementation | Required pass/fail checks and recovery |
|---|---|---|
| TEX-PKG-01 / accepted stage1; inspect `runtime/chrome-extension/{native-workspace,compiler,offline-store.browser}.mjs` and actual compiler asset interfaces (confirm paths first) | Open Packages for a project; resolve package-file names and transitive dependencies against trusted version-compatible metadata. Show installed, missing, unsupported, size and offline readiness. Record the selected resource path/manager and supported package types here. | PKG-T01: bundled project remains offline-ready; missing `.sty`/`.cls`, font/data and transitive/cyclic/unknown dependencies are handled without hanging or silently choosing a different engine/version. Dynamic/ambiguous requirements stay explicit. Missing catalog/network retains cached metadata and compilation. On unsupported add-on loading, keep readiness diagnostics usable, record the limitation and stop dependent installation rather than claim success. |
| TEX-PKG-02 / TEX-PKG-01 and supported browser resource API; existing compiler/store/UI, no native runtime required | Select Install in the app, approve the bounded package/dependency set, stage download, verify trusted digest/signature/compatibility and license, safely unpack into an app-owned versioned tree, then atomically activate. Cancel/retry without discarding usable resources. | PKG-T02: install a compatible package absent from the baseline bundle, compile it with its dependencies, then disable networking, reload and compile again with exact source/PDF navigation. PKG-T03: wrong hash, incompatible version, corrupt archive, traversal/absolute/symlink path, oversized/decompression-bomb input, executable/post-install hook, offline miss, quota exhaustion, interruption and concurrent install/compile all refuse safely. Previously active resources/source/PDF remain intact. Upgrade/retry cannot mix versions or invalidate an in-flight result's resource manifest. Rollback switches the active resource pointer to the verified prior set; no originals overwritten. |
| TEX-PKG-03 / TEX-PKG-01/02 plus optional selected stage3 native helper; `runtime/chrome-extension/native/`, native compiler/bridge and package UI | Where supported, offer the same action for the selected native installation through its existing manager with fixed arguments and app-owned/user-writable package tree. Display which local installation changes; collaborator requests cannot initiate it. | PKG-T04: a supported missing native package and dependency install/compile/reopen through the UI. Missing manager, non-writable tree/admin requirement, version skew, manager failure, cancel and two simultaneous requests preserve the previous installation and give actionable status. No root escalation, system-tree mutation, document-derived command or inherited credentials/network access in the compile sandbox. If the manager cannot uphold this boundary, mark that native target unsupported and stop that path; browser support remains available. |
| TEX-PKG-FINAL-01 / TEX-PKG-02; TEX-PKG-03 required only for advertised native support | Deliver the packaged native UI flow, licenses/source provenance, safe project resource manifest and offline-readiness status. Public resources may be cached separately from restricted project data. A peer may report requirements but cannot force download or execution. | PKG-T05: project import/join/revocation never installs automatically; hostile package names/URLs/metadata rejected; restricted project/source markers absent from cache/logs; same public package bytes reused without duplicate per-project storage. Uninstall/update refuses to break an active compile and warns about projects whose readiness changes. PKG-T06: repeated install/cancel/retry/use/export/reopen on synthetic projects; offline/edit/export/LAN/P2P regressions for modes advertised with this increment; exact runtime/resource versions and footprint documented. Failure leaves feature unavailable and preserves existing editing/compiler/resources. |

**Performance and integrity:** use the same synthetic project/machine before and after in this milestone's single check pass. Record first install time, transferred/extracted bytes, peak memory/disk, first and warm visible PDF time, installed artifact size and repeat-install network/storage writes. Initial unmeasured budgets: no repeated payload download/write for an already verified identical package; no more than10% warm-compile regression on the representative fixture (report noise/sample counts); disk growth bounded by the declared package set plus one staged/rollback set, with staging removed after verified activation. Record misses and profile the dominant cause before extra optimization. Cold install time depends on selected package size/network; establish a labeled target after the resource interface is known rather than fabricate a measurement. Active compile inputs include exact resource versions/digests; incomplete installs are never marked offline-ready.

**Not allowed / stop points:** no package-manager command typed by the user, mandatory external manual installation, automatic privileged installer or research-environment modification. Ask before any first external package-source contact or change to a selected user-owned TeX installation, after presenting the exact supported action and rollback; do not use a feasibility limitation as permission to replace the compiler. Unknown licensing/provenance, lack of safe transactional storage or required privileged code blocks installation support for that target.

**Acceptance and accounting:** all PKG-T01–T06 scenarios applicable to the advertised target must pass using the real application. Unsupported targets are explicitly excluded, never counted as passed. ### S6-04 design — save the project to GitHub and continue from it (user, 24 November 2026)

**Goal (user's words: "saving the project's things to the GitHub file (like tex, pdf and such) to make other users gain the files again from GitHub and continue the work").** This is project saving, not a room backup: a user saves the project's files to a GitHub repository; another user opens the project from that repository and continues; their next save goes back to the same repository. It works for any project, with or without a shared room. Nothing is implemented; facts marked *unverified* must be checked against GitHub's current documentation first.

**What is saved.** The project as it is in the editor: all source and asset files (`.tex`, `.bib`, `.sty`/`.cls`, figures, data) at their project paths, plus the latest compiled PDF of the main document (e.g. `output/<main>.pdf`; exact location to decide). Not saved: compile caches, logs, SyncTeX scratch, room passcodes/keys/identities, GitHub tokens. A small marker file records the main document and compiler so "Open from GitHub" restores the same settings.

**User flow (recommended).**
1. **Save to GitHub** (button, per the user's decision): first time, sign in (GitHub device code) and pick or create a private repository; then every save is one commit ("Saved by <name>, <time>") with the changed files and the current PDF. If the repository changed since this computer last opened or saved it, the save stops and shows an explicit merge (`p2p-reconcile.mjs`, both versions kept) instead of overwriting.
2. **Open from GitHub**: another user signs in, picks the repository (or pastes its URL), and gets the files as a new local project, PDF included; they edit and compile as usual and Save to GitHub again.
3. Inside a shared room, the same button saves the room's current files; it never stores room secrets.
4. **Offline edits (user, 24 November 2026: "we shall be able to push to github of the offline edits as well"):** editing works fully offline as today; the project remembers the GitHub commit it was last opened from or saved to. Saving while offline is kept as pending local work (the editor's own copy), and the next "Save to GitHub" once online pushes all edits made since that commit. If the repository moved on meanwhile, the explicit merge (both versions kept) runs against that remembered commit as the common base; nothing is overwritten on either side.

**Account options (still open, user decides later).**
| Option | How it works | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| A. One repository in the owner's account, others as collaborators (recommended) | The project's owner creates the private repository and invites others by GitHub username; each signs in with their own account. | GitHub enforces who can read/write; history shows who saved what. Others need a GitHub account and accept the invitation once. |
| B. Each user's own repository | Each saves to their own repository and shares it. | Copies drift apart; continuing means finding the newest one. |
| C. A shared token built into the extension | — | Not allowed: anything in the extension is public. |
| D. Secret gist | — | Unlisted, not private; only its owner can write. Unsuitable. |

**Sign-in (no server).** Recommended: a GitHub App registered for this project, signed in with the device flow (only the public client ID ships; no secret) and installed only on the chosen repositories *(unverified: device-flow setting, calls from an extension page with a github.com host permission, token lifetime)*. Not recommended: an OAuth App (`repo` scope covers all private repositories) or pasted personal access tokens.

**User decisions, 24 November 2026 (Europe/Madrid).** Content readable in a private repository (plain files, normal commits) — an explicit user-approved exception to stage 4's no-third-party-storage rule, limited to repositories the user chooses. Saving only by the explicit button (no automatic saves). Sign-in each time, token in memory only, nothing reusable on disk. **Deferred by user, 24 November 2026: "skip the github part for now".** Account model and app registration remain undecided; S6-04 is excluded from this delivery and keeps zero credit.

**Checks (when built).** Save, open on a second profile, edit, save back, reopen first: identical files and PDF. Edit offline (network off), then save once online: every offline edit reaches the repository; with a concurrent save by someone else, the merge keeps both. Conflicting saves produce the explicit merge. A user without access is refused by GitHub. The commit contents contain no token, passcode, room key or cache; the user's secret/provenance scan runs on the pushed tree.

**Weight.** 18 (GitHub App and device flow, repository/collaborator handling, save/open with PDF, conflict detection and merge, permission and secret checks).

### Rules for every agent (each one is a stop condition)

1. **Definition of working.** A working state is reached only when the user does its named actions in a browser against a running instance. A README reading, an audit table, a validator pass or a planning-asset build does not advance anything.
2. **Run first, audit second.** Nothing in CAP-01, WEB-01, AUTH-01, EDIT-01, STORE-01 or JOB-01 is done from documentation while WS1 is not running. Each is performed on the running instance.
3. **No documentation-only or tooling-only sessions.** Do not add planning assets, validators, schemas, status fields or plan restructurings. A session that ends with only Markdown or planning tooling changed has failed, unless the user asked for exactly that.
4. **Do not build what upstream already does.** Before writing any code, show on the running instance that the capability is missing or broken, and record the exact action that failed in the WS2 table. Do not replace a functioning editor, OT algorithm, preview, compiler or queue. Add only the helper/registration needed by the current stage, with its actual permission and packaging checks.
5. **Unmodified first.** WS1 and WS2 run the pinned upstream distribution with configuration only: no patch, no fork, no copied source. The licence decision in section 2 is therefore needed before WS3's first source change, and not before. Confirm the licence text at the pinned commit when WS3 starts; do not assume it.
6. **One working state at a time, in order WS1 → WS6.**
7. **Stop and ask the user (do not guess) when:** a container or server is about to be started for the first time; a port would be reachable from outside this Mac; an external integration, real account, real project or credential would be used; source would be modified or copied; a timebox is exceeded twice.
8. **Unchanged and still binding:** synthetic accounts and projects only, work outside Kod, no user data or credentials in plans or logs, originals recoverable, phases pass only on real evidence.
9. **Build first, check after (user, 15 November 2026: "plans first done and then checks; finished as soon as possible").** Inside a working state the order is: get the whole state working, then run its checks and gates once, then fix what they found. The step tables in section 3 (WEB-01, AUTH-01, EDIT-01, STORE-01, JOB-01) are the checks run after the state works; they are not preconditions for starting it. Do not insert feasibility campaigns, audits, matrices or 30-minute discovery checkpoints between implementation steps. The only checks that come first are the ones in rule 7 and rule 8.
10. **Checkpoint entries are at most six lines:** what the user can now do, exact pinned identities, what failed, next action.

## 1. Product, scope and completion

Their VS Code APIs cannot simply run in a browser: equivalent web workflows need a proven CE+ feature, compatible component or explicit integration work.

## 2. Architecture and reuse rules

Start from CE+'s existing browser experience, not a new reduced editor shell. WEB-01 must establish whether additions can use existing integration points or require a small, maintained source-derived patch. The prior prohibition on any server/UI adaptation cannot force removal of requested functionality; document the minimum integration approach and update/rollback burden before implementation. Prefer upstream-compatible changes and preserve upstream UI/features. This planning task does not create a fork or change licenses.

Keep browser editor/collaboration code in its existing JS/TypeScript host language and reuse the tested upstream state machine. Use Rust for custom backend persistence/recovery/scheduling components only where an existing service does not suffice. Do not rewrite the editor, transforms, preview engine or compiler solely to make them Rust. Intended areas, subject to WEB-01 source discovery: existing CE+ web editor/components, a narrow browser integration layer, custom Rust service/storage if needed, and upstream Toolkit/CLSI queue/worker configuration. Do not invent final source paths before inspecting the pinned source tree.

**Local only** must remain usable without contacting the remote collaboration server, publishing presence or submitting remote jobs. A locally served web UI may use a loopback helper for local files/toolchains if browser APIs cannot meet the requirement. Ordinary stage 1 editing/PDF/export needs no helper. Direct LAN transport may require a helper; stage 3 workers need a qualified local tool boundary. Distinguish remote-server isolation from loopback access. Browser filesystem permissions, supported import/export, refresh/close behavior and offline startup remain checks; local compilation/autocompile is now required in stage1; optional installed-TeX worker distribution remains stage3. If the chosen browser cannot safely support a workflow, record the gap and investigate the smallest alternative; do not silently drop local operation.

**Connect to server** uses the designated deployment's browser login/session and project permissions. Prefer ordinary same-origin session authentication and upstream SSO rather than importing full browser cookies into a client. Do not transplant VS Code SecretStorage, extension activation, `workspace.fs` or VSIX packaging requirements into the web design. Browser-facing remote-service adapters must keep server secrets out of frontend code and storage.

Licensing is an unresolved integration prerequisite. Existing original documentation remains under the repository's current license. Record exact revision, notices, modifications, source delivery and distribution boundaries before incorporating upstream code; the proprietary notice does not relicense upstream material. Do not assume process separation, plugin invocation or a private deployment resolves source obligations. If the reuse strategy conflicts with the current license, present the concrete decision before source incorporation. No license change or new upstream source incorporation is authorized merely by this documentation revision. Preserve the existing native integration source and notices.

### 2.1 Reuse existing PDF components and planning

**Objective and boundary:** reduce duplicate PDF work by retaining CE+'s preview first and adapting existing Data File Viewer (DFV) components only for demonstrated gaps. This addition authorizes planning only: no source incorporation, runtime dependency, license change or application acceptance. TeX source remains authoritative; editing a compiled PDF does not update collaborative source or replace SyncTeX.

**Evidence inspected on 10 November 2026:** local DFV source at `f5d5b73d2077d58665b4603cc9d37b71bc9daa7d` and Python PDF UI source at `4e7eb4e7ca295430a717841f2c2039ef5491b882`.
