## 4. Editing, session and recovery safeguards

Show Local only / Connect to server explicitly for each browser workspace session. Test refresh, restored tabs and saved settings for unintended remote contact. Local only does not send remote authentication, presence, telemetry, source or compile jobs. An ordinary local helper binds only loopback; an explicitly activated LAN helper binds only the chosen private interface and authenticate/origin-check requests and avoid exposing files/commands to arbitrary sites. Browser downloads/imports or helper permissions must never silently grant arbitrary filesystem access.

Use upstream browser session authentication on the intended same origin, secure transport and supported cookie settings. Keep authentication tokens out of URLs, localStorage, source files, frontend bundles and logs. Preserve upstream CSRF, authorization and XSS boundaries; enforce project/role checks server-side on every edit, asset and job/result route. If a separate Rust service needs an adapter, qualify its exact authentication/origin boundary before access. Do not scrape browser profiles or collect raw full-access cookies as the default web login flow. Logout/revocation stops authorized server operations while retaining recoverable candidates according to explicit retention policy.

Pin origin/account/project/document identity, confirmed base/version, editor revision and tracking state. Persist exact outgoing intent before transmission where the participant has permission to retain data; for host-online-only LAN participants keep intent in bounded session memory and enforce lock/reconciliation on host loss. Atomically record definitive confirmation/base advancement at the authoritative owner. Distinguish durable, submitted, confirmed, ambiguous and held states. A queue/transport acknowledgement or matching text is not proof of application. Resolve uncertain submission through authoritative history/identity; if proof expires, preserve the candidate and hold rather than blind replay. Failed storage or revoked permissions stops sends.

Preserve dirty editor buffers, persisted local candidates, last confirmed base and current remote state separately. Hydrate/import through staging; never silently replace a user folder, buffer or asset. Diff/reconcile in the web UI with explicit copy/export/manual choices. Reject path traversal, symlink/reparse escapes in any helper, archive path escapes and case collisions. Do not infer remote delete/rename from absent local files. Preserve original `.bib` keys/comments/order, figures including PDF inputs, styles and data; do not run generators or conversions automatically.

**R1 no-write data-loss regression:** edit a browser buffer and independently modify its persisted candidate/local file; change the same file in another authenticated browser; disconnect/reconnect, refresh and restart. Include dirty-at-initial-hydration, permission failures and remote rename/delete. All candidates remain recoverable, divergence is visible and no remote mutation is emitted. The Workshop overwrite issue is a regression pattern, not proof that this web path has the same bug.

Before accepting LAN writes, qualify review-preserving concurrency, supplementary Unicode/UTF-16, line endings, undo/redo, IME, multi-cursor and replacement/formatting races in real browsers. Unknown review/format state stays held/read-only with preserved candidates. That is a temporary safety limit and open capability gap, not permission to claim untested review actions. Stage1 must expose usable local offline recovery; stage2 adds owner/authorized-copy recovery; automatic uncertain merge/replay remains gated on proof. Exercise account/project switching so pending content never crosses identity boundaries. Closing the browser never stops the independent shared server.

## 5. Compilation and shared job distribution — stages 3 and 4

Stages1/2 provide local compilation/autocompile without a shared worker pool. Stage 3 uses optional participants who explicitly select a local TeX installation and opt into contribution; the host coordinates without a TeX requirement. Stage 4 does not use this machinery: each peer compiles its own PDF locally (decision 20 November 2026). Do not silently select a second compiler architecture.

Give each job a durable identity scoped to account/project, immutable source snapshot/digest, compiler/toolchain/recipe and options. Queue admission and worker dispatch must survive restart or fail visibly with recoverable source. Use the actual upstream lease/claim model where sufficient; recover abandoned work without duplicate publication. Compile execution may retry, but only the authorized current job/generation may publish the result. Reject stale/foreign results, duplicate completions and results arriving after cancellation/revocation. Bound retry counts and expose terminal failure. Any caching/deduplication must include permissions and full input/toolchain identity; preserve upstream caching safely without requiring a new cache.

Workers receive only permitted job inputs and limited credentials; separate per-job directories, sandbox resource/network limits, restricted result channels and retention/cleanup are required. Establish a disposable restore path before queue/storage configuration changes. Source originals remain unchanged. TeX and optional Python execution are trust boundaries, not ordinary trusted text processing. No secrets or private payloads in status/metrics/evidence. Backup/restore covers project data and queued/running job reconciliation without restoring revoked access.

| Step / dependencies | Workload, actions and measurable acceptance | Failure/recovery action |
|---|---|---|
| JOB-02 / JOB-01, stage 3 opted-in workers and qualified queue identity | Use 4 synthetic accounts, 3 projects and 2 isolated workers; submit 12 short fixture compiles plus 2 deliberately slow jobs from one account. Observe per-user/project progress, queue/status latency, bounded workers, CPU/RSS and exact source/PDF mapping. Implement the complete workflow, then compare the existing/candidate routes on the same fixtures in the check pass. | Every admitted job gets a correct result or visible bounded failure/cancellation, and no account monopolizes available dispatch slots. Repair fairness/backpressure before distributed-work acceptance; never weaken correctness to meet speed targets. |
| JOB-03 / JOB-02 | Kill a worker during compile, duplicate/delay completion, cancel/revoke while queued/running, restart scheduler and restore a disposable queue snapshot. Attempt foreign-project submission/result access, malformed payloads and result/path escape. | No lost admitted job, unauthorized data/result, stale publication or unbounded retry; abandoned work is retried within configured limits or visibly fails. Restore the pre-change queue/config snapshot and preserve sanitized failure evidence if identity/recovery fails. |

**Initial performance targets, unmeasured:** on a declared small-team host, job admission p95 ≤500 ms and visible status update p95 ≤2 seconds, excluding upload/compile time; at most one simultaneous job per user while another user waits for available capacity. Start with two workers, one active job per worker, 60-second fixture timeout and at most one retry. With non-hung workers, each waiting account must receive a dispatch opportunity within two configured job timeouts. Record actual compile distributions, queue wait, RSS/CPU and hardware before setting production CPU/RAM limits or capacity promises. These are feasibility targets to validate, not measurements or a mandated production timeout. If missed, record the cause and revise topology/capacity or the explicit performance proposal; retain fairness, isolation and correct ownership. No universal speedup claim: queue distribution helps concurrency but does not guarantee faster compilation of one document.

## 6. Incremental delivery and execution policy

There are five product stages after the package-management addition; stage5 uses the supplemental PKG checklist above. Stable schema-3 internal checkpoints R0–R7 remain for evidence compatibility, grouped as stage1 R0–R2, stage2 R3–R4, stage3 R5, stage4 R6–R7; they are not eight product releases. First publish depends only on offline stage1 gates; LAN has separate stage2 gates. Versions describe truthful scope, not promised dates. Missing checks remain pending rather than copied from a prior architecture.

Shared runtime-step contract: record actual working directory/environment/commands and inputs before execution; implement the complete milestone, then check and repair; update status/evidence/remaining limits afterward. Preserve recoverable source and previous configuration; stop dependent acceptance on failure and roll back only task-owned changes. No fixed test quotas or speculative platform claims. Keep all test scripts, reusable fixtures and recorded reference results locally as requested; source/archive verification does not authorize their deletion.

## 7. Verification and deliverables

Test inherited behavior with integration active; reuse upstream tests and synthetic fixtures where applicable. Scope checks to changed functionality, trust boundaries and known regressions. Real browser/server/worker evidence is required where specified; mocks, skips and README claims cannot pass those gates. Browser refresh/termination, permission/quota/storage failure and multiple sessions are required in addition to actual Chrome-extension installation/package checks for current delivery. Preserve archived native-host findings as historical evidence only.

Application deliverables are versioned web source/assets and the actual required service/container/configuration artifacts, dependency notices, pinned runtime/toolchain identities, checksum/provenance and rollback instructions. R0 must implement this actual packaging/smoke pipeline; current scripts still produce planning assets only. No VSIX or Marketplace deliverable. Do not deploy, publish releases or activate external integrations under this documentation task. A source commit/push is distinct from deployment or release.

Repository documentation/tooling verification, from an outside-Kod temporary checkout containing committed tests and intended inputs: `npm ci`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run validate`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:package`. The builder requires a clean committed snapshot. All must exit zero and reopen six planning assets; they prove documentation/validator/package consistency only, not a web app, supported browser or server capability. Check `git diff --check`. There is no plan mirror: this is the only plan (Kod copies removed 21 November 2026 at the user's request); do not recreate one. Keep tests, reusable fixtures and recorded reference evidence as the user requested. Remove only disposable browser profiles/registrations and unrelated reproducible outputs after preserving reference results; never delete the only copy or unfinished work.

## 8. Current evidence and limitations

Current verified state: stage1 offline gates R0–R2 pass for the declared Chrome/macOS arm64 target, indexed by actual browser/artifact evidence. Native editor, autocompile/current PDF, files/preferences, exports, light/dark/keyboard, fault/crash/quota recovery and installable artifact/source/security/license checks passed. Later LAN/research/online stages remain unaccepted; no browser/OS/physical-accessibility coverage beyond the declared evidence or public binary release is implied.

The 7 November experiment demonstrated bounded Rust + VS Code + unchanged CE+ engine behavior. Its archived exact identities, measurements and failures are retained in section 11. The web-interface decision supersedes its extension-based delivery design: do not ship its client as the product or count extension assertions as browser acceptance. In particular the whole-document replacement/range-mapping failure, supplementary Unicode, review races, storage loss and cross-platform gaps must be investigated in the chosen browser path.

## 9. Release gates and phase execution

The existing schema-3 validator uses R0–R7 internal checkpoints. They are implementation checkpoints within the four product stages, not eight separate product stages. Stable gate IDs are retained to keep evidence and validator references valid; their current criteria below replace the old decisions. `docs/phase-status.json` contains no passing phase evidence. First offline publish targets R2 only after R0–R2 pass; LAN stage2 targets R4, optional computation stage3 targets R5 and internet stage4 targets R7. Never mark a skipped/mock-only check passed.

### Phase R0 — Stage 1 offline foundation

CEX-EDIT-01/CEX-COMPILE-01: open/write/compile in the native offline workspace without a separate system TeX/helper/network requirement; record actual source/browser/assets and dependency licensing.

### Phase R1 — Stage 1 local project integrity

CEX-EDIT-01: exact files/preferences/PDF/export, guarded atomic writes and explicit recoverable conflicts.

### Phase R2 — Stage 1 offline publication checks

CEX-FINAL-01: complete native offline UI/autocompile/generated-PDF/recovery coverage, installable offline-only artifact, real repository/security/license/device/provenance checks. No LAN requirement.

### Phase R3 — Stage 2 LAN collaboration

CEX-LAN-01: authenticated encrypted join, native text/undo/carets/project transfer and safe disconnect/rejoin.

### Phase R4 — Stage 2 owner permissions and LAN publication

CEX-LAN-PERM-01/CEX-LAN-FINAL-01: host-online-only versus authorized independent copies, transport/storage/export enforcement, real LAN package and device checks.

### Phase R5 — Stage 3 optional local computation and LAN compilation

LAN-COMPILE-01 plus RESEARCH-RUNTIME-01/MATH-CHECK-01/DOCEXEC-01/ANALYSIS-01: optional participant TeX distribution and local Python/R/Jupyter integration, prioritized bounded math diagnostics and executable documents, validated outputs and safe failures; tools remain optional and the host needs no TeX.

### Phase R6 — Stage 4 internet rooms and signed ownership

P2P-ROOM-01/P2P-OWNER-01, required scenarios P2P-T01–T04: link join across different networks without an application server, verified snapshot, encrypted native convergence, direct/approved relay failure handling and owner-signed membership/transfer/backup/revocation. Invitation URLs contain no secret.

### Phase R7 — Stage 4 coordinator handover and internet publication

P2P-HANDOVER-01/P2P-FINAL-01, required scenarios P2P-T05–T08: loss-free coordinator handover, split-brain/fork reconciliation, permission-bound offline local copies and packaged two-network physical-device/security/licence/performance checks. Stage5 has its own supplemental PKG-T01–T06 acceptance and does not add an internal R8.

### Native/compiler comparison reference — 15 November 2026, 20:19 CET

The user is right to reject 0.63–0.72 s as Word-like. Directly changing where the same batch compiler runs is not enough. One bounded same-Mac comparison used identical source/three same-width edits/assets, fixed SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH, pdfTeX 1.40.29/TeX Live 2026 and the current final Linux image. Native macOS missing packages/fonts were supplied as 266 platform-independent files (~5.49 MB archive plus one bitmap font) in a private isolated tree; no installed toolchain/environment was modified. First observed runs include different initial font/cache states and are not fair cold-start rankings. Warm numbers below are two repeats, engine-only, not visible editor latency.

| Route | First observed engine run | Two warm engine runs | Conclusion |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| ARM Linux VM, ordinary pdfTeX | 1,936 ms | 757 / 760 ms | Full-document processing remains costly. |
| Native macOS, ordinary pdfTeX | 836 ms | 802 / 804 ms | Local native execution alone was not faster in these warm samples. |
| ARM Linux VM, preamble reuse | 977 ms | 325 / 322 ms | Current batch approach already removes much package work. |
| Native macOS, preamble reuse | 1,042 ms | 264 / 264 ms | About 60 ms saved at the engine; no installed end-to-end improvement demonstrated. |

Native Mac warm peak process RSS was about 33 MiB; first format creation peaked near 40 MiB. VM peak memory was not measured in this review. Mac full/reused outputs match all 3,588 extracted word geometries to one another, but each differs from Linux on one word geometry; native Mac replacement is not fidelity-qualified. Tectonic 0.17.0 is installed but its offline run lacks exam.cls; no valid performance result, network fetch or live adoption. Comparison source and isolated inputs remain under /Users/macc/overleaf-vital-20261115/native-review, protected 0700, required by this active unresolved compatibility checkpoint. The live application/image/mounts were not changed during this review.

## 11. Detailed source and regression references

The following source inspection, native-host experiment and checkpoints are retained for provenance and reusable engine/failure findings. References to VS Code, SecretStorage, extensions, installed-host checks, VSIX, browser handoff and prior scope exclusions describe the former design. They do not override sections 1–10, establish web qualification or authorize extension implementation. The current product uses the native browser UI, packaged as a Chrome extension for standalone delivery; the abandoned VS Code-client design is not a requirement. Requalify applicable findings in actual browsers. Private experiment links remain pointers only; no private source, credentials or runtime data is imported here.

### Verified reuse limits — 3 November 2026

Read-only source inspection at Workshop commit `835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d` found the following. These are source findings, not installed-host or live-server acceptance:

| Boundary | Actual upstream behavior | Smallest safe next step |
|---|---|---|
| Extension interface | `src/extension.ts::activate` returns no API object. `src/core/remoteFileSystemProvider.ts` registers the `overleaf-workshop` filesystem and the internal `remoteFileSystem.prefetch` command, which returns a mutable VFS. | Prefer standard `workspace.fs` reads for an isolated probe; do not couple the companion to internal VFS objects or claim a supported authentication/project API. Prove activation side effects before connected use. |
| Cookie storage | `src/api/base.ts::Identity` contains cookies; `src/utils/globalStateManager.ts::loginServer` persists that identity in `context.globalState`. The cookie input in `src/core/projectManagerProvider.ts` does not request password masking. | Unmodified upstream login does not satisfy our SecretStorage/masked-input boundary. Do not delegate our login to it, import its persisted credentials or claim SecretStorage in our companion fixes upstream storage. Use browser handoff until a compatible secure adapter is proven. |
| Mirror direction | `src/scm/localReplicaSCM.ts` writes local hydration/merged content and can call `workspace.fs.writeFile` on the remote URI. The filesystem provider also exposes create/write/delete/rename. | Do not invoke Open Locally or attach its replica settings for R1. Probe read operations in a fresh disposable profile/project, preserve candidates in our staging area, and independently prove zero remote writes. Calling a read method alone is not proof of read-only session behavior. |
| Local-only activation | `package.json` activates Workshop for its URI scheme or `.overleaf/settings.json`; `activate` can initialize a saved replica. | Use a clean local-only workspace/profile without replica settings for the first isolation test. Later exercise saved-settings/restart cases explicitly; our Local only choice cannot promise to disable an independently installed extension. |

Pinned source references: [activation](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/extension.ts), [manifest](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/package.json), [filesystem](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/core/remoteFileSystemProvider.ts), [credential persistence](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/utils/globalStateManager.ts), [identity](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/api/base.ts), [login input](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/core/projectManagerProvider.ts), [replica](https://github.com/overleaf-workshop/Overleaf-Workshop/blob/835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d/src/scm/localReplicaSCM.ts).

**Result:** retain the unchanged CE+ browser and full LaTeX Workshop workflows as baseline capabilities. Workshop remains a source/protocol reference and a candidate read provider, not an accepted secure/read-only adapter. Resolve missing interfaces through a proven adapter or an explicitly licensed reuse path; do not invent another OT algorithm or treat a fallback as final parity. These 3 November findings describe source inspection only; the separate 7 November PoC reused pinned engine/transport code as recorded below. No production safety gate is weakened.

### Independent R2 feasibility — 7 November 2026

**Conditional go for Rust + VS Code using the existing CE+ collaboration engine.** This is an isolated experimental result, not a production release or acceptance of any R0–R7 gate. The production-qualified compatibility list remains empty. The separate PoC is complete and archived privately; no application implementation is authorized by this document consolidation.

The [complete technical assessment, executable PoC and synthetic evidence](https://github.com/cenkeryildiz/kod_phases/blob/c6b7e2e6b81483ea0d47ca2deebcc4d979a716f3/experiments/r2-poc/FEASIBILITY.md) are retained at private commit `c6b7e2e6b81483ea0d47ca2deebcc4d979a716f3`, branch `work/r2-poc-20261107`. The archive's README gives reproducible setup and validation commands. Runtime secrets, generated builds, synthetic server data and completed disposable checkouts were removed after remote verification; reproducing the experiment requires a fresh isolated environment.

| Tested component | Immutable identity |
|---|---|
| CE+ source | `ae07e781ab20dac2378420e5cf7a47f7d8bccbb5` |
| CE+ arm64 image | `sha256:c8844bd6ca900d78a5187793a99a30553d77ec2f399d49f8b00edb29d46487a9` |
| Overleaf Workshop reference | `835a488a7eac760fa2d74685cf1072f95393b30d` |
| LaTeX Workshop reference | `2f5a60a11a99f33013c1235f0499546cc831cfcb` |
| Toolkit | `26f13760e04e5b66e6bcd344295d6525e390e88f` |
| Reused Socket.IO client | `805a73d2a2e2408982597d5986a401088b7aa588` |

Actual host: macOS arm64, VS Code 1.145.2, isolated profiles and synthetic accounts/projects. CE+ and its collaboration protocol were unmodified. The exact upstream ShareJS engine and RangesTracker were reused with Workshop's existing transport patch. The current handshake uses `projectId` and `joinProjectResponse`; do not assume the old explicit joinProject/connectionAccepted path exists.

| Requirement | Experiment result and boundary |
|---|---|
| Native VS Code editing and two-client concurrency | Verified for basic insert/delete, remote editor updates and BMP text such as café, Greek and Chinese. No new OT algorithm. |
| Rust durability before transmission | Verified: an independent journal process read the exact committed submission before network emission; a failed journal prevented sends. Confirmation records follow definitive applied events, not the earlier queue callback. |
| Lost confirmation / termination / expired history | Verified safe-hold outcomes. Applied-event suppression, whole Node-client SIGKILL and actual missing history after 110 operations preserved exact pending intent without duplicate replay. Actual VS Code main-process termination is not yet qualified. |
| Reconnect | Clean reconnect and subsequent confirmed edit verified. Ambiguous pending work is held; seamless automatic uncertain recovery is not implemented. |
| Review metadata | Verified custom tracked insertions, hidden deletions, author identity and shifted browser comment anchors on the exercised ShareJS configuration. No native review UI or continuously refreshed native range model. |
| Permission revocation | Actual collaborator downgrade stopped writes; local candidate retained and server readback excluded it. Broader revocation races remain pending. |
| Unsupported format / characters | Actual `history-ot` document join was refused without content/version changes. Supplementary Unicode was retained and refused without submission. This proves safe refusal, not full support. |

The experimental boundary was **VS Code/TypeScript for editor APIs and the reused JS/TS state machine; Rust for custom backend persistence and recovery safeguards; unchanged CE+ for transforms, versions and review processing**. This is reference evidence, not the current delivery architecture. Persist stable document/account/origin identity, exact engine state, tracking seed, submitted connection identity and exact outgoing message before emitting. On unknown status, retain and hold. Workshop filesystem writes cannot supply this boundary. Do not port a complete OT engine to Rust.

Final small loopback run: 28 ms concurrent convergence; maximum observed Alice journal commit 273 microseconds. Local journal assertions sampled median 73/max 178 microseconds over 20 commits. The browser baseline took about 1.9–2 seconds and missed the initial one-second investigation target; upstream scheduling differs from the prototype's immediate durable queue. These measurements are not large-project, throughput or resource certification.

**Outstanding threats:** stock-browser whole-document replacement followed by concurrency produced an unresolved range-mapping error. Full supplementary Unicode/history-OT support, native undo/redo/IME/multi-cursor races, disk-full/power-loss behavior, remote authentication/TLS and Windows remain unqualified. Those experimental client results establish no current stage acceptance; qualify the current browser/room boundaries before publication. Full findings A–F and historical failure evidence remain in the linked assessment.

The archived PoC's supplementary Unicode, undo/IME, replacement-race, disk-write failure, auth and review tests remain regression references for the actual browser/LAN integration. Do not resume the abandoned VS Code product/installer or require its host as a shipping dependency. Preserve its archived tests/evidence and independently qualify current browser behavior.

### Reference — Optimization programme — user direction 2026-11-18 (expands the OPT rows above; this table is authoritative)

**Rules for every OPT step (user):** improve the application; reuse existing components; no new compiler survey or speculative engine migration. Local compiler/PDF tasks apply to stage1; shared-pool/internet tasks apply to stages3/4; editor/storage/PDF/transport tasks apply to stage 1 as relevant. No dependency without measured need. Preserve LaTeX compatibility, exact source, PDF fidelity (output equal to a clean compile), collaboration, offline, authorization/revocation and project isolation. Accept only a measured end-to-end user-visible gain on the same representative fixture and machine with no correctness/durability/security/usability regression; unit tests, bundle size, dependency count or engine-only time are not evidence. Where the engine cannot support an optimization safely, keep the full correct path. Each step: find the existing implementation, confirm its bottleneck, smallest complete change, then one check pass. **Not allowed:** weakening authorization caches, public deployment/provider activation. **Stop and ask:** hosting/provider/account choice, anything that pauses a user installation, deleting the only copy of any data.

- What the user can now do: reopening or reloading a connected project whose sources have not changed shows its PDF in about 1 s instead of ~5 s, and the editor's automatic duplicate compile after a manual Recompile no longer occupies a device. host.mjs keeps per project+user the latest committed {digest, result, output dir}; a queue request with `reuse: true` whose snapshot digest matches and whose output is retained >60 s returns that result immediately, refreshes retention to 1 h and discards that user's older jobs (same as a newer queue superseding them). client.mjs sends `reuse` only for automatic compiles (bootstrap.js reads CE+'s `auto_compile=true`; on-load and on-change), returns a reused result without polling, and for automatic compiles asks before waiting for its own compiler (waits only if no result). ## Stage 3 MATH-CHECK engine checkpoint — 20 November 2026, Europe/Madrid (Claude; independent of stage1/stage2 integration)

- **Built (standalone, not integrated):** `runtime/math-check/mathcheck.py` (364 lines, one file, JSON stdin/stdout for the future native bridge; `python -I`) and `test/math_check_test.py` (18 tests). It uses the user's own Python and SymPy; without SymPy it answers `unavailable` and installs nothing. Document text never reaches eval/sympify/parse_latex: a small recursive-descent parser builds SymPy objects and refuses unsupported or ambiguous notation with a source range. The Python used to check it is the unrelated `image_resizer` env (SymPy 1.14.0, read-only use); `myproject` has no SymPy and was not changed.
- **Checked:** `<python-with-sympy> -B -I test/math_check_test.py` 18/18, 1.7 s. Mutation checks (disabled counterexample search, domain check, relation guard, sampling signs, literal-zero guard) each fail a test. Measured: 38-case corpus 0.59 s total, slowest 0.07 s, no false positive on 23 true identities; this is not a universal accuracy claim.

Objective: an already-open internet room should tolerate a brief reported RTC disconnection, then enter existing loss/rejoin/election safely when the disconnection persists, rather than wait for a much later browser `failed` signal. Keep pending edits/owner grants/membership checks and wire compatibility unchanged. Scope: p2p-transport.mjs, its existing p2p-room tests and this plan. Current base/main0c05ea26daa1aa1a98cf2f6bbec43daeea771ac0; isolated `/tmp/latex-phase4-continue-20261125`, branch work/phase4-recovery-20261125; original dirty Kod source and current installation/release remain untouched.

Choice: reuse RTC connectionstatechange plus one cancelable3sgrace timer per disconnected peer. Existing WebRTC already supplies this signal; new heartbeat packets would change the framing protocol/mixed-version compatibility and add periodic traffic, so they are unnecessary here. No dependency, new configuration, interval, secret, external service or paid usage. This bounds recovery from the reported disconnected event, not from physical packet loss; election/discovery timing and suspended-tab scheduling remain separate unqualified limits. Investigate/implement within30minutes, then one scoped check pass; if it cannot be implemented safely, keep current runtime and record the limitation.

- P4R-01 (in progress): add shared connection-state watcher; both owner and participant use it. Stable disconnected/connecting after the deadline closes the transport via existing paths; connected cancels; repeated disconnected events cannot extend the deadline; failed/closed end immediately. Clean timers/handler before teardown, refusals and shutdown so no late events act on another session. Read all current callers first (two transport adapters, lan-guest and p2p-room recovery layers). Preserve existing authentication, framing/backpressure and pending-edit logic.

### QNAV-20261127 — question-text double-click investigation (user report)

User report, verbatim: "when I double click some words in the question part, it sometimes wrongly jumps in the tex part. Note this into the md file and try to find the reason first".

Status: recorded; cause unverified. Investigate first, with no new navigation patch until the failure is reproduced or the mapping defect is identified. Use a copy of the existing Downloads template in a disposable profile; originals/profile untouched and no document text in logs/fixtures/GitHub. Distinguish literal question-body words from generated question numbers/labels, macro expansions and solution text. Compare PDF word geometry, backward SyncTeX source line and native editor selection; classify wrong-line vs wrong-word vs generated-label behavior. Reuse current parser/viewer and original fixtures, no dependency/compiler comparison. Timebox initial diagnosis 20 min; report reproductions and limits before deciding a fix. Native-host acknowledgement changes remain saved, unbuilt/undelivered in /Users/macc/latex-native-host-20261127 (two modules/two tests); no live test process or lock. Next: inspect existing inverse-selection tests/parser, collect synthetic and copied-template question-only click mappings without source logging.

- QNAV diagnosis checkpoint (27 November 2026, Europe/Madrid): settled 21 copied-template clicks on words in question-prefixed PDF spans; every viewer hint contained the clicked word and none was incorrectly changed to a question-number hint. Seven had no exact selection: six words absent from the corresponding question source (generated metadata; do not misclassify as literal-body failures), one repeated word on the correct source line. Confirmed root behavior independently against the installed native editor handler with a synthetic question: a word occurring twice on one logical TeX line returns a cursor at column 0, whereas a unique word selects exactly. The installed handler uses indexOf(selected) === lastIndexOf(selected); the custom refine core returns null on repeated ordinary words and the SyncTeX route supplies a line without a column. Three question source lines exceed 200 characters, so logical-line fallback can look like a large jump among visually wrapped lines. This is a verified cause of apparent wrong-position jumps, not proof that every user-reported case has this cause.
- A separate synthetic edge exists in PDF label detection: its numeric-prefix hit area is hard-coded to 2 × font size and can consume a short first body word; not reproduced in those 21 template clicks, so not asserted as this report's cause. Broader question-body probe first stopped for offscreen-coordinate harness trouble (no result accepted); corrected probe scrolls each target into view, current session 93956. No navigation source changed. Original template/profile unchanged; source text never logged. Next: classify broader settled clicks, record result/remaining limits, then complete the already-saved native-host acknowledgement independently.

- **QNAV diagnosis result — 27 November 2026 (Europe/Madrid), reproduced before any navigation edit:** copied-template probe restricted to words occurring once as lexical tokens in the entire source: 36 actual question-word clicks, 36/36 correct logical TeX lines, 35/36 exact word selections. The remaining literal word occurs once as a token but twice as a substring on its line; native code treats that as ambiguous and leaves a cursor at column 0. Independently reproduced against the installed editor handler with synthetic "rate" / "corporate": the unique standalone word is rejected because indexOf/lastIndexOf also sees it inside the longer word. Thus two confirmed causes of apparent wrong-position jumps are real repeated words and false repeats inside longer words/command text. SyncTeX returns no column; the custom refine core defers ordinary words to a native substring-uniqueness check, whose failed selection falls back to the start of the logical line (possibly several wrapped visual rows away). Relevant paths: runtime/synctex-selection/refine.js sameLine branch, runtime/synctex-selection/apply.mjs editor hook, installed js/codemirror-editor-57ce7aa576296ae31fad.js editor:gotoLine handler; native-workspace.mjs supplies line-only inverse results.
- The broader mixed-word probe included generated/solution text, so it is not accepted as an oracle for question-body source-line accuracy; only the unique-token 36-click result above is used. No wrong-logical-line case reproduced in that sample; this does not rule out others. Separate numeric-label 2-em hit-area edge remains synthetic only. **No navigation fix or source reformatting made**, per user request to diagnose first. Next repair, if resumed: boundary-aware literal matching, then a separate safe rule for truly repeated occurrences using geometry; never choose the first occurrence arbitrarily. Native-host fix checks independently pass: focused 30/30, repository 296 pass/1 existing skip, real Chrome negative control emitted the unchecked error and four handled missing-host operations emitted zero; pending archive/delivery.
